Items marked `[x]` are resolved on live evidence and kept for history; orchestrators only need to act on `[ ]` items.

- [x] id: 20261017-1 | severity: high | target: test-agent | kind: destructive_git | **RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (mdu-47)**
  - evidence: mdu-23 (2026-10-17, opencode harness): the test-agent ran `git checkout -- .` and discarded the uncommitted MDU-23 edits (the session-hygiene prompt gates), then restored them. The working tree held the only copy of the implementation at that moment — a restore was needed and happened to succeed. `prompts/test-agent.md`, `grok/agents/test-agent.md`, and `grok/agents/test-agent-pro.md` contain no destructive-git guard, and the orchestrator's destructive-ops approval gate (orchestrator.md L68) names deletes/migrations/irreversible ops but not git state commands, so a test agent inferred `git checkout -- .` was routine cleanup.
  - definition change: In base prompts/test-agent.md and base grok/agents/test-agent.md (and test-agent-pro.md mirror) add: "Never run destructive git state commands — `git checkout -- .`, `git restore .`, `git reset --hard`, `git clean`, `git stash drop`, `git checkout <ref> -- .` — on a dirty tree. The uncommitted working tree is the source of truth for the MDU under test; test in place, or copy the tree / use a worktree if a pristine checkout is required. If you accidentally revert state, restore from reflog/stash immediately and say so in the verdict."
  - apply-now: In the next test-agent brief, state explicitly "do not run git checkout/reset/clean; the working tree contains uncommitted MDU edits; test in place or in a copied worktree." Have the orchestrator commit (or `git stash create`) before delegating to the test agent so a snapshot always exists.

- [ ] id: 20261021-2 | severity: high | target: opencode-harness | kind: upstream_bug
  - evidence: mdu-30 validate (2026-10-21): QA found that `cd <dir> && <cmd> >/path` bypasses all six redirect-deny wall entries of the new per-agent shell allowlist for any absolute target — suspected harness-level handling of the `cd` special case strips the redirect before the permission match runs. Without the cd prefix the wall denies correctly (re-verified live in validate_round2), and the form pre-existed MDU-30 (no redirect rules existed then), so this is an unclosed hole in the new control, not a regression. It is config-unfixable at our layer: no allowlist ordering can enforce against a stripped redirect. Disclosed in docs/opencode-v2.md and pinned by a disclosed-hole test so it cannot silently widen.
  - definition change: none at our layer — the wall cannot be repaired from opencode.json. Related optional hardening logged by review round 2 (additive `*/dev/null *>*` / `*/dev/null*>*` wall entries against null-sink laundering of an absolute redirect) is still unapplied and cheap if a follow-up MDU touches the wall.
  - apply-now: File the upstream OpenCode bug report for the cd-prefix redirect bypass — still unfiled as of this introspection — with the minimal repro (`cd /tmp && echo x >/etc/whatever` style probe) and the observed vs expected permission match. Until it is fixed, treat absolute-path stdout redirects as unenforceable whenever a `cd <dir> &&` prefix is present, keep the Hole 8 disclosure in the docs, and do not claim the wall covers that form in any review.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-31): still unfiled, and MDU-31 changed the *authority* to file it: the new repository-ownership hard rule makes upstream issues/PRs on third-party repos an explicit per-action human approval, never a default MDU step. The apply-now is now gated on the user saying "file it" for this specific report — an orchestrator must not file it off this line alone. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-15): still unfiled — deliberately. This MDU's conformance driver hit a *different* reproducible endpoint failure in the same headless-invocation area: an unpinned opencode leg resolves to the dead free alias `flint-40b-free`, so the driver now pins `--model "${OPENCODE_MODEL:-opencode/big-pickle}"` (see new 20261021-10). Two distinct headless-invocation defects now on record in this area; neither is filed upstream, and both remain gated on an explicit per-action user instruction naming the repo and the action. Left open.
  - note (2026-11-01, mdu-50): still unfiled — no change; still gated on an explicit user instruction naming the repo and the action (repository-ownership rule). Not a default MDU step. Left open.
  - note (2026-11-02, mdu-52): still unfiled — no change; still gated on an explicit user instruction naming the repo and the action (repository-ownership rule). Left open.

  - note (2026-11-11, mdu-58): still unfiled — gated on an explicit user instruction naming the repo and the action (repository-ownership rule); no new repro this run. Left open.

- [x] id: 20261021-3 | severity: low | target: session-introspect-skill | kind: detector_gap | **RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (mdu-50)**
  - evidence: mdu-30: the manifest records two consecutive review rejections from different reviewer variants (code-reviewer-pro REJECT round 1, code-reviewer REJECT round 2, then code-reviewer-free APPROVE round 3), and fix_iterations=2 surfaced the retry cycle, but session_signals.py reported review_rejections=0 — the streak counter appears to require the same agent name to repeat, so a reject→fix→reject cycle that rotates reviewer variants (pro → mid → free) reads as zero. Introspection then under-counts review friction on any multi-variant review pipeline.
  - definition change: none — tooling, not an agent prompt.
  - apply-now: When a brief rotates reviewer variants, derive the rejection count from the manifest phase outcomes rather than the script metric; a script fix would group agent names by family prefix (`code-reviewer*`) before counting a streak.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-31): no recurrence test — this run had a single reviewer round and a single APPROVE, so review_rejections=0 was correct. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-09): confirmed a second time — the manifest records two review phases with outcome "fail" (code-reviewer-pro REJECT round 1, code-reviewer REJECT round 2) while the script reported review_rejections=0; this run's rotation was pro→mid (an availability failure, not a variant cycle), yet it is the same blindness: the streak counter cannot see a rejection from a differently-named reviewer. The manifest-derived fallback did fire correctly (`phase_fail_streak` "review failed 2 times"), so the gap is confined to the rejection counter. The apply-now (derive rejections from manifest phase outcomes) is what this record uses. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-24, mdu-33): no recurrence test — single reviewer round, one APPROVE, review_rejections=0 was correct. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-26, mdu-35): **confirmed a third time** — the manifest records a review phase with outcome "fail" (code-reviewer REJECT on the inert `('QUESTION TOOL',)` tuple) followed by a code-reviewer-free APPROVE, while the script reported review_rejections=0 (mid → free variant rotation). The manifest-derived fallback also stayed silent because no single phase name failed twice (review 1 fail, validate 1 fail). This record's apply-now (derive rejections from manifest phase outcomes) is what was used: 1 rejection this run. Left open pending the family-prefix counter fix.
  - note (2026-10-27, mdu-34): no recurrence test — single reviewer round, one APPROVE, review_rejections=0 correct. Left open pending the family-prefix counter fix.
  - note (2026-10-30, mdu-40): no recurrence test — the script's review_rejections=0 came from the stale mdu-20 tree (20261021-4), not this run; the manifest shows one base review (sign-off, pass) and one overlay review (2 findings fixed) with no REJECT, so 0 was coincidentally correct but unverifiable from the script. Left open pending the family-prefix counter fix.
  - note (2026-10-31, mdu-45): blind spot silently confirmed — the script reported review_rejections=0 while the manifest review phase records a genuine REJECT round (1 MAJOR vacuous census-wiring test + 1 MINOR tautological assert) before the APPROVE; true count 1 from the phase note, per this record's apply-now. No streak fired, correctly — it was one rejection, not consecutive same-name rejections — but the counter again cannot see a single REJECT→fix→APPROVE cycle. Left open pending the family-prefix counter fix.
  - note (2026-11-01, mdu-47): blind spot silently confirmed — the script reported review_rejections=0 while the manifest review note records a genuine round-1 REQUEST CHANGES (2 MAJOR: tautological D1b ordering pin, missing backlog ruling+follow-up lines) before round 2's APPROVE; true count 1 from the phase note, per this record's apply-now. Neither counter could see it: the script needs 2+ same-name rejections, and the phase outcome reads "pass" (round 2), so the manifest-derived fallback is silent too — the merged-rounds form first seen on mdu-41. Left open pending the family-prefix counter fix.
  - note (2026-11-01, mdu-50): **RESOLVED — the tooling fix this record asked for shipped in mdu-50, and this run's design corrected the record's own mechanism diagnosis.** (1) The family-prefix counter fix is live: `_family()` pools tier variants in the streak detector (streak_agent = _family(agent), variants listed in evidence), `reject` added to REJECTION_PHRASES so bare REJECT verdicts match, and family-pooling extended to repeated_tool_error / context_reread / qa_fail_repeat — grep-verified in the working tree AND in both installed copies (~/.config/opencode + ~/.grok skills, 11 fix-marker hits each; devops verified 3/3 cell markers byte-identical at deploy). Tests pin it: pro REQUEST CHANGES → mid REJECT → free LGTM fires the streak under agent `code-reviewer` naming both variants, bare-REJECT ×2 pins the phrase. (2) Mechanism correction, per docs/design/mdu-50-session-signals.md fact 1: the streak loop NEVER had a same-name gate — rejections from any variant already accumulated — so this record's original diagnosis ("requires the same agent name to repeat") was wrong; the real blindness was (a) bare `REJECT` not in REJECTION_PHRASES (fixed) and (b) v2 rows carrying empty text so no text detector can fire at all on a v2 tree (out of scope, §9.1). (3) Residual symptom this run is cause (b), not a recurrence of the family gap: the script reported review_rejections=0 while the manifest records a genuine round-1 REQUEST CHANGES (true count 1 from the phase note) — and a direct probe of this run's tree proved why: 0 of 216 events carry any text (the v2 collector sets text=""), so `_is_rejection` can never match on the current store. That structural limit is now tracked as new 20261101-3 (v2-no-text detector inertness), not left implicit here. Apply-now going forward: derive rejection counts from manifest phase notes on opencode runs until 20261101-3 lands. Closed — the family-prefix fix this record requested is shipped, tested, and deployed; the live-count blindness has its own entry.

- [x] id: 20261021-5 | severity: low | target: session-introspect-skill | kind: detector_gap | **RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (mdu-50)**
  - evidence: mdu-31 stale-run output (see 20261021-4) reported "qa reported FAIL 2 times" while the actual QA phase was a single GRADE PASS. Traced to the detector: `_is_qa(agent) and "FAIL" in text` counts any QA message whose part text contains the substring `FAIL` — the two hits were the QA prompt's own verdict-format sentence ("End with exactly: QA GRADE: PASS or QA GRADE: FAIL") quoted back in QA messages, not failures. Any QA agent that quotes its instructions trips the threshold.
  - definition change: none at agent-prompt level — tooling. Match a verdict, e.g. `re.search(r"QA GRADE:\s*FAIL", text)` counted once per message, or ignore occurrences inside quoted instruction text.
  - apply-now: When the script reports qa_fail_repeat, confirm the QA phase outcome in the manifest before routing anything to test-agent; a quoted "…or a QA GRADE: FAIL" instruction line is not a failure.
  - note (2026-10-27, mdu-34): no signal fired (no qa_fail_repeat). Left open.
  - note (2026-10-30, mdu-40): the stale run reported qa_fail_repeat "2 times" on the wrong tree (mdu-20) — the known detector reading a stale session; no real QA FAIL this run (QA PASS with 3 defects, all fixed). Left open.
  - note (2026-10-30, mdu-41): a related blind spot, no count change — the manifest records ONE review phase with outcome "pass" whose notes describe an initial sandbox-only REJECT followed by an APPROVE with no changes. So neither the script counter (review_rejections=0, requires 2+ same-name rejections) nor the manifest-derived fallback (no review phase with outcome "fail") can see it; an environmental REJECT that the orchestrator overrode without a fix cycle is invisible in phase outcomes. True count this run: 1 (from the phase note). Left open pending the family-prefix counter fix plus recording overridden REJECTs as their own phase outcome.
  - note (2026-11-01, mdu-50): **RESOLVED — the substring detector this record diagnosed shipped its line-anchored fix in mdu-50.** `QA_FAIL_VERDICT = re.compile(r"(?im)^qa grade:\s*fail\b")` replaced the bare `"FAIL" in text` test: a message quoting the format instruction mid-line ("… QA GRADE: PASS or QA GRADE: FAIL.") no longer counts, a standalone verdict line counts exactly once per message, case-insensitive, `\b` rejects FAILED — grep-verified in the working tree AND both installed copies (2 QA_FAIL_VERDICT hits each), documented in SKILL.md:90, and test-pinned three ways in tests/test_session_signals.py (quoted-instruction negative, standalone-once positive, lowercase positive), mutation-killed in the test phase (4/4). This run: no qa_fail_repeat fired, but that is NOT live confirmation — QA passed on the first verdict (nothing to repeat) AND v2 trees carry no text at all (see new 20261101-3), so the detector could not fire either way; the closure rests on the code + tests, which is what this entry's definition change asked for. Residual kept as documented in the design (§6): a message quoting the bare verdict token alone on its own line still counts — the apply-now (confirm the QA phase outcome in the manifest before routing) remains the backstop. Closed.

- [x] id: 20261021-8 | severity: high | target: qa | kind: permission_thrash | **RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (mdu-49)**
  - evidence: mdu-15 validate (2026-10-21, opencode, session ses_ea049984fffeJ40fe7IUhOoZw0): a QA subagent's `shell` call was permission-denied, and instead of retrying it searched the Code Mode tool catalog for a shell tool and concluded it had none — a false capability conclusion. The deployed allowlist does grant QA a usable form: `timeout *` is an allow entry in every agent block (docs/opencode-v2.md:67; the same session's other legs ran commands behind `timeout <n> <cmd>` successfully, including this MDU's grok conformance leg from a QA subagent). The misdiagnosis cost real time: qa ran 1281s active / 54 events (long_cycle HIGH) and 168,073 tokens (12%, token_hotspot warn), the second-largest hotspot of the run, largely in denial-workaround turns. This is the same agent family as the closed 20261018-1 but a different failure mode: that one reported BLOCKED, this one concluded "no shell tool exists"; neither the closed suggestion's text nor the deployed prompt names the `timeout` wrapper form.
  - definition change: In base prompts/qa.md and the QA mirrors add: "If `shell` is permission-denied, do not conclude that shell is unavailable. Retry once with the allowlisted wrapper form `timeout <n> <command>` (e.g. `timeout 120 python3 -m pytest -q`) — bare `shell` may be denied while the `timeout *` allowlist entry is open. Only if that form is also denied, fall back to file tools plus orchestrator-attached outputs, labelling those checks 'verified via orchestrator-attached output'."
  - apply-now: In the next QA brief, state the workaround verbatim — "bare `shell` may be denied; the allowlisted form is `timeout 120 <cmd>` — retry with it before reporting BLOCKED or 'no shell tool'" — and route any command that runs an arbitrary script (driver.sh, deploy probes) to devops or the orchestrator, handing QA the produced artifacts to grade.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-11): no recurrence — no permission_thrash signal on qa this run; the two QA children (validate + re-validation) ran a full 6-across-AC pass and a finding-by-finding re-check with shell-dependent checks done (gate probes, greps, suite counts) and neither reported BLOCKED or a capability misdiagnosis. qa's long_cycle HIGH (1406s / 66 events) and 162,923-token hotspot this run are workload (two full validates), not denial churn. The definition change remains unapplied but the harness-level fix (20261018-1 closure) continues to hold. Left open pending post-definition-change evidence.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-09): no recurrence — no permission_thrash signal on qa this run; the QA child graded AC-1/AC-2/AC-3 (including a live 3-task promptfoo run) to GRADE PASS with no BLOCKED report and no capability misdiagnosis. qa's long_cycle HIGH (1079s / 62 events) and 126,429-token hotspot are workload (CLI cross-checks plus live gate demos), not denial churn. - [ ] id: 20261021-9 | severity: warn | target: orchestrator | kind: phase_routing
  - evidence: mdu-15 (2026-10-21): the design's phase-6 instruction "qa runs the conformance driver" was structurally impossible under the deployed harness — the `qa` agent's shell permission is deny-by-default with a narrow inspection allowlist (docs/opencode-v2.md:65-67), so QA cannot execute an arbitrary script. The impossibility was discovered during execution, not at design or review time, and required a post-hoc routing correction recorded in the design doc's post-implementation notes (lines 462-469) and docs/observability.md: devops (unrestricted shell) or the orchestrator executes the driver, QA grades the artifacts; the grok leg only ran from a QA subagent because `timeout *` happens to permit a wrapped command. The same class of mismatch (a phase assigned to an agent whose allowlist cannot run it) is what generated the QA churn in 20261021-8, and MDU-30's pre-deploy reviewer hitting the same wall is a third instance of designs/briefs assuming permissions the target agent does not have.
  - definition change: In base prompts/orchestrator.md, base grok/agents/orchestrator.md, and overlay grok/agents/grokestrator.md add: "Before delegating a phase, check the target agent's shell allowlist in opencode.json against the commands that phase requires. A phase that must execute an arbitrary script or an unrestricted command routes to devops or runs in the orchestrator session; QA and other inspection agents receive the produced artifacts to grade. Do not assign a phase to an agent whose allowlist cannot run its commands — verify the permission profile at design time, not mid-run."

- [ ] id: 20261021-11 | severity: warn | target: code-reviewer | kind: prompt_contract_trace_gap
  - evidence: mdu-11 (2026-10-21, opencode): the commit-gate clause added to all three orchestrator mirrors specified a fallback procedure, but the missing-scanner fallback was ordered AFTER the exit-code semantics — so an agent following the text would run an absent script, get exit 2, and escalate instead of applying the checklist. code-reviewer-pro (premium) APPROVED with all 10 mandated checks PASS, explicitly including "all 5 D6 elements are in both orchestrator mirrors and the overlay's condensed line carries all five behaviours" — i.e. it verified element PRESENCE, not the order an executing agent would traverse. The first QA pass (GRADE PASS) also passed it. Only the devops live deploy — actually running the commands from the overlay repo position — surfaced the defect, costing fix cycle 2 (reorder in 3 files plus an explicit "a missing script is a fallback, not an escalation" sentence) and a full re-test/re-validate round. The same class recurs: the review's mandated-check format encourages presence-tick semantics ("is clause X present in all mirrors") while procedure clauses are only correct in ORDER and in execution context. A premium reviewer plus a mid QA both missed a defect that a live run caught in one step — the cheapest check (execute the documented path once) was the only one that worked.
  - definition change: In base prompts/code-reviewer.md (and the grok reviewer mirrors) add: "When a mandated check verifies prompt TEXT (a clause, a gate, a fallback), trace it as a procedure, not a presence tick: in order, from the position of an agent in each repo variant (base and overlay), what does the text make you DO when the primary path is unavailable? A clause whose ordering sends the executing agent down the wrong branch fails the check even when every element is present. State the trace in one line per variant in the verdict." Mirror the same sentence into base prompts/qa.md and the QA mirrors for acceptance criteria that cover prompt text.
  - apply-now: In the next review brief for any MDU that adds or edits prompt clauses, replace the presence-style mandated check with a trace-style one: "for each new/changed clause, write the one-line execution trace an agent in the BASE repo and one for the OVERLAY repo; flag any trace where ordering changes behaviour." Additionally, for prompt-text-only changes, keep a live-execution phase in the loop (devops or QA running the documented command from the overlay position) — mdu-11 shows this is the only check that caught the defect. No definition file has been edited; this is brief-side only.
  - note (2026-10-21, mdu-09): **EXTENDED and confirmed on a second run.** This MDU carried two REJECT rounds, and each found what the previous round had missed. Round 1 (code-reviewer-pro, premium) caught behavioural defects — F1 the promptfoo config did not actually run, F4 secret_scan's git-SHA blind spot. Round 2 (code-reviewer, mid), reviewing after fix cycle 1, caught 3 new MAJOR defects that were ALL on error paths: a snapshot-write OSError handler that itself crashed (it passed an invalid `reasons=` kwarg to `_fail`), the baseline anti-tamper check vacuous under a hand-edited non-pass value, and the diff step doing no snapshot-shape validation — plus 2 MINOR (absolute target path escaping the repo root; tab-indented YAML accepted) and 6 NITs. A presence tick ("handler present", "check present") passes every one of those; only tracing the failure path exposes them. So the blindness generalises: it is not just ORDERING (the mdu-11 case) but error-path execution of procedure text generally. Confirmation from two directions: (1) even a premium reviewer's mandated-check format missed error-path defects that a mid reviewer caught on the second pass — no single review round was sufficient; (2) the gate this MDU ships IS the countermeasure — ordered-trace golden cases (order matters, presence does not), a committed baseline with anti-tamper, and a fail-closed exit contract, i.e. the trace discipline this suggestion prescribes, automated — and QA validated it live (reorder → exit 1, tamper → exit 2, missing case → exit 1). The definition change is still unapplied; the strongest available argument for applying it is that the tooling now exists and the review format still does not. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-24, mdu-33): **first run where the apply-now worked within a SINGLE APPROVE round.** The review brief used trace-style mandated checks (manifest: "trace-style checks 1-6 executed") and the one-round APPROVE still surfaced 4 MINORs whose substance was error-path: absent-model not failing closed, and explicit `--variant zen` absence not exiting 1 — both presence-tick-blind defects, both fixed in cycle 1 alongside a passthrough allowlist check, a top-level parity assert, and doc-count/README corrections. Compare the series: mdu-11 (premium reviewer APPROVED with the ordering defect in place; caught only by live execution), mdu-09 (two REJECT rounds before error-path MAJORs surfaced). Here the trace discipline caught error-path defects BEFORE approval, in one round, from a mid reviewer at 61.9k tokens — the strongest evidence yet that the definition change (trace-style, not presence-tick, mandated checks in prompts/code-reviewer.md and the QA mirrors) is worth applying. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-27, mdu-34): **third consecutive run where trace-style review caught an error-path defect pre-approval.** The T1–T8 static-trace single pass APPROVED but flagged the 1 LOW: `resume_block.py:138` UnicodeDecodeError uncaught → a raw traceback instead of the contracted lowercase `[fail]` line — exactly the kind of failure-path defect a presence tick passes (file present, tests green), fixed in cycle 1 by a fresh coder child. Series now: mdu-33 (error-path MINORs inside one APPROVE), mdu-35 (REJECT on the inert tuple), mdu-34 (LOW on the error path). Definition change still unapplied; three consecutive runs are the argument. Left open.
  - note (2026-10-29, mdu-42): **fourth consecutive run where the trace-style finding caught an execution-path defect pre-approval.** Round-1 REQUEST CHANGES flagged F1 MAJOR: the probe-gate xai wiring was dead code — no `--config` passed, results computed but ignored — exactly the "wired-in-text but inert at execution" class a presence tick passes.   - evidence: mdu-09 (2026-10-21, opencode): the review required execution evidence — proving the F1 "promptfoo config did not run" and F4 "secret_scan git-SHA blind spot" findings meant actually running the new gate and the scan — but review agents are sandboxed read-only and cannot run python/shell, so the ORCHESTRATOR ran the execution-evidence battery itself into `/tmp/opencode/mdu-09/review-evidence.md` and handed the file to the reviewer (recorded in both REJECT phase notes). The routing is 20261021-9's remedy applied at brief time and it worked, but the cost landed on the most expensive agent: the orchestrator posted a series-record 4018s active / 412,077 tokens (see 20261014-1/-2 notes), and the reviewer was bound to a pre-computed snapshot it could not refresh when fix cycles changed behaviour, so each re-review round needed a fresh hand-off rather than a re-run.

Optional config hardening if a follow-up MDU touches the wall: grant reviewers a scoped allow for the single named evidence script (e.g. `timeout 60 python3 scripts/lib/prompt_regression.py gate ...`) instead of general shell, so a reviewer can refresh evidence itself without gaining a write primitive.

Pass the file path to every review round, and re-run the battery after each fix cycle rather than asking the reviewer to infer what changed. Prefer routing the battery to devops over the orchestrator: the orchestrator doing execution work is the same pattern as 20261014-1.

- [ ] id: 20261026-1 | severity: high | target: opencode-harness | kind: upstream_bug
  - evidence: mdu-35 (2026-10-26, opencode): session-storage forensics over the inline-part era found 42 question calls in the sprite_generator sessions — 30 completed, 12 error. Primary mode (15 of 18 machine-wide inline-era error calls): an unanswered pending question is hard-killed at 60.0–61.0 minutes ("Tool execution interrupted", finish:error, idle interrupted) with no requeue and no auto-reprompt; a same-payload retry 5.4 minutes later in the reordering session succeeded, proving the question payload was fine. Secondary benign mode: 3 schema-shape rejections the model self-corrected. Agent definitions and config were NOT defective. Mitigation shipped this MDU: the QUESTION TOOL discipline clause byte-identical in all three orchestrator mirrors (ask early, batch, post-pause dead-question check, abort-restatement hard rule) + 2 validator fuse tuples + a pinned adjacency test + 16-case suite (471 passed), deployed live to opencode/grok/claude. The upstream-issue draft appendix in docs/design/mdu-35-question-tool.md is NOT filed.
  - definition change: none — the prompt-side mitigation already shipped in all three mirrors via mdu-35.
  - apply-now: Treat any question left unanswered across a ~55-minute pause (or after any harness restart) as dead: re-ask it instead of waiting, and never stack a new question late in a long session — the deployed clause carries this, so the next brief only needs to confirm the block is still live in the deployed copies (grep each of ~/.config/opencode, ~/.grok, ~/.claude). The upstream report stays DRAFT and may be filed only on an explicit user instruction naming the repository and the action (repository-ownership rule); never file it as a default MDU step.
  - note (2026-10-27, mdu-34): **the primary kill mode recurred live.** Two question calls in mdu-34's earlier sessions were hard-killed at the ~60-minute pending mark; both were answered inline by the user with no run damage — a second independent confirmation of the 60-min hard-kill after mdu-35's forensics, this time in sessions running after the mdu-35 clause was deployed (whether those specific turns followed the clause's ask-early/batch discipline is not verifiable from this tree). The deployed clause mitigates ordering but cannot prevent a kill of a question still pending at 60 minutes. The upstream draft remains unfiled, still gated on an explicit user instruction naming the repo and the action. Left open.

- [ ] id: 20261030-2 | severity: warn | target: code-reviewer | kind: env_failure_as_verdict
  - evidence: mdu-41 (2026-10-30, opencode): the code-reviewer's shell was hard-denied mid-loop, and its first verdict was a REJECT on purely environmental grounds (manifest review note: "initial REJECT was sandbox-only (reviewer shell denied); orchestrator ran all 4 gates green … verdict APPROVE (no changes)"). An environmental limitation was reported as a review outcome: it cost a full review round plus the orchestrator absorbing the 4-gate battery itself (the 20261021-12 recurrence), and no code defect was ever involved — the second round approved with zero changes. The reviewer is read-only by design, so this denial is expected behavior of the sandbox, yet neither the reviewer definition nor the brief tells the child how to treat it. QA hit the SAME denial on the same run and behaved correctly (graded PASS, labelled the execution-blocked items as confirmed by orchestrator runs — 20261021-8 mdu-41 note), so the better shape exists in the loop; it just is not in the reviewer text.
  - definition change: In base prompts/code-reviewer.md and the grok reviewer mirrors add: "Your shell is denied by design (read-only sandbox). Never issue REJECT/BLOCKED because a command could not run. Try one command; on denial, note 'shell denied: <cmd>' in one line and complete the review as static-trace over the diff. If execution evidence is genuinely required, return 'EVIDENCE BLOCKED: <commands needed>' as a note for the orchestrator to satisfy — an environmental block is never a code verdict."
  - apply-now: In the next review brief, state up front: "shell is denied by design — scope this review static-trace; gate outputs are attached at <path>; report any denial in one line and never as REJECT." Carry 20261021-12's pre-dispatch evidence rule at the same time (name the evidence producer BEFORE dispatching the reviewer). Together these surface the denial at first contact instead of after a rejected round.

Second consecutive clean run after mdu-43 under the same brief shape. Definition change still unapplied — brief-side discipline is holding. Left open.

  - evidence: this session (mdu-46/47/48 span) produced TWO edit-tool corruption incidents: (1) mdu-46's manifest edit corrupted the JSON on disk (caught by QA, repaired by the implementer — already on record in 20261014-5's mdu-46 note); (2) during mdu-48 introspection prep, an edit RESULT's echoed text showed a phantom phase row "Deprecating live artifact registry" in a full-file write's oldString-mismatch error — verified by git-grep this run: the string exists NOWHERE on disk or in the working tree, i.e. the corruption was DISPLAY-ONLY (the tool's result text was wrong; the disk was never touched). Never propagate edit-result display text as truth. The standing 'validator after EVERY manifest edit' rule covers the manifest side; this covers the general case."
  - apply-now: In the next brief involving manifest or config edits, add one line: "if any edit result looks wrong, verify the file on disk (grep/read) before using it as evidence." Cheap; catches the display-corruption class at first contact.

This introspection's own compound shell form (`cd && grep`) was permission-denied by design and the fallthrough to file tools was the same verify-on-disk shape the clause mandates. The same class is cheap to repeat: notes asserting "definition change unapplied" are inherited from previous notes, not re-verified.
  - definition change: In the introspector prompt add: "Before recording that a definition change is unapplied (or letting that claim drive a closure decision), grep the target file(s) at HEAD for the clause text and cite the commit hash that added or last changed it.   - apply-now: At the next introspection, for every suggestion about to receive a "definition change still unapplied" note, run one grep per target file first; on any mismatch between the claim and the files, correct the log in the same pass. This run's two closures (20261017-1, 20261026-2) were both gated on exactly that grep.

Shell was available only in plain `timeout N <cmd>` form this run (a compound `cd && grep` was denied by design), so the grep cites are on-disk with the commit hash deferred to the commit phase, exactly as the clause's shell-denied fallback prescribes. The clause's value is now proven on the exact failure class that opened it (the 15-note stale claim on 20261017-1). Left open pending post-commit evidence.

Dogfooding this run went beyond mdu-49's: before writing any status note the introspector (1) grep-verified every target clause in the working tree AND at HEAD and cited the commit hash (2fff3f8 mdu-50 design, 2fa753f mdu-49, 428b505 overlay, 4dac611 last pre-mdu-50 script commit), (2) corrected rather than repeated the six mdu-49 "commit pending" notes once `git log` showed the batch had landed, and (3) proved this run's review_rejections=0 was NOT the20261021-3 family-prefix gap but a new root cause (v2 empty text: 0/216 events), opening 20261101-3 instead of re-inheriting the old diagnosis — exactly the "correct the record with evidence, don't repeat the claim" behavior the clause mandates. - [x] id: 20261101-3 | severity: high | target: session-introspect-skill | kind: detector_gap | **RESOLVED 2026-11-01 (mdu-51)**
  - evidence: mdu-50 (2026-11-01, opencode): a direct probe of this run's tree (`_collect_opencode` + `_compute_metrics` on the root session id) returned 216 events with **0 events carrying any text** — the v2 collector sets `text=""` for every event (session_signals.py:1081) and never populates tool/tool_path/tool_error/permission_requested either, so on the current live store every text- or tool-based detector is structurally inert: `review_reject_streak`, `qa_fail_repeat`, `repeated_tool_error`, `context_reread`, and the `review_rejections` metric all read zero regardless of what happened. Live proof this run: the manifest records a genuine round-1 REQUEST CHANGES (1 MAJOR) before round 2's APPROVE, yet the script reported `review_rejections: 0` — and unlike20261021-3's historical blind spots, this is NOT the family-prefix gap (that fix shipped and is grep-verified); it is that `_is_rejection` never sees the verdict because the text is empty. Same for permission_thrash: this introspector hit ~8 permission rejections this run and the script could not count them. The mdu-50 design knew the text half (fact 1b, §9.1 "v2 text extraction — out of scope, follow-up candidate gated on a live-store schema probe") but the scope note covers only text; the missing tool/permission fields are the same class and are not named anywhere in SKILL.md, which documents these signal kinds as if they work. Until extraction lands, the script's JSON silently under-reports exactly the friction the introspector exists to catch (review rejections, QA repeats, tool errors, permission thrash) on the primary harness.
  - definition change: none at agent-prompt level — tooling + skill doc. Two parts: (a) SKILL.md must disclose the limitation where the signal kinds are listed — "on OpenCode v2 stores the collector extracts tokens/model/timestamps only; text-, tool-, and permission-based kinds (review_reject_streak, qa_fail_repeat, review_rejections, repeated_tool_error, context_reread, permission_thrash) read zero by construction — treat them as UNKNOWN, not clean, and derive review/QA friction from manifest phase notes" — and/or the script should emit a note (`"v2 store: text/tool/permission detectors inert"`) whenever it selects v2 so a zero cannot masquerade as a clean run; (b) the design §9.1 follow-up: probe the live `session_message`/part schema and extract text (and tool/permission parts if present) so the detectors work again.

- [x] id: 20261102-1 | severity: high | target: session-introspect-skill | kind: detector_gap | **RESOLVED 2026-11-03 (mdu-53)**
  - evidence: mdu-52 (2026-11-02, opencode): the first run AFTER the matcher hardening reports `review_rejections: 3` plus a `review_reject_streak` HIGH ("3 consecutive rejections from code-reviewer") on a run whose manifest truth is 0 rejections (both rounds APPROVE). Direct probe of this tree: the 3 matching code-reviewer messages are (1) a verdict-less mid-review analysis message whose prose contains "it's not a rejection", (2) a message whose verdict line reads `## Verdict: APPROVE` but whose body quotes the regex source `reject(?:ed|s|ing|ion|ions)?` and writes "a reviewer discussing the detector is not rejecting", and (3) the cycle-2 message with verdict line `## Verdict: APPROVE (cycle-2 delta)` whose body quotes guard branches and example strings (`rejections? <op> <digit>`, `"rejections from the two rounds: REQUEST CHANGES"`). The word-boundary matcher correctly matches these as words; the four guards cover the signal kind, "no/zero rejections", standalone-quoted phrases, and quantified-metric templates, but not prose negation ("not a rejection"/"not rejecting"), verdict-less analysis messages, or longer quoted example strings. Compounding bug: `_is_approval` requires `not _rejects(vt)`, so an APPROVE-verdict message whose body discusses rejection vocabulary BOTH increments the streak AND fails to reset it — the FP both fires and sticks (hence a false HIGH streak from 3 messages across 2 rounds, whose own evidence string contains "## Verdict: APPROVE"). The mdu-52 live probe only tested mdu-51's session (reads 0 — 20261101-4's class is fixed, live-exercised); it never tested a session where the reviewer reviews the matcher itself, and tests/test_mdu_52_rejection_matcher.py's 15 cases pin none of the three live FPs above. This class recurs deterministically on any MDU that touches session_signals.py (mdu-50/51/52 all did).
  - definition change: none at agent-prompt level — tooling (session_signals.py) + one test file. Give verdict lines precedence: when a reviewer message contains a standalone verdict line (`## Verdict: …`, or a line starting with REJECT / APPROVE / REQUEST CHANGES / LGTM), classify the message by that line alone — body prose must never override or veto it; a message with no verdict line (mid-review analysis) is not a rejection or approval round. Extend the negation guard to "not a/n rejection(s)" and "not rejecting". This resolves both the FP increment and the approval-veto in one rule; reconcile with the mdu-52 strictly-narrowing invariant (verdict-less messages flip True→False by design) and re-pin the affected tests. Required pins: a reviewer message analyzing the matcher (quoting REJECTION_RE, `reject(?:ed|s|ing|ion|ions)?`, "it's not a rejection", example strings) WITH an APPROVE verdict line must read review_rejections=0 and must count as an approval (no veto, streak resets); a verdict-less analysis message must not count as a round; a REJECT / REQUEST CHANGES verdict line must still count.
  - apply-now: until fixed, treat `review_rejections > 0` or any `review_reject_streak` on opencode as unverified — trace the matching event (one `_collect_opencode_v2` + `_is_rejection` probe) and trust the manifest phase notes (the 20261101-4 standing rule, now covering the prose/quote class too). Cheap tell: a streak whose evidence string contains `## Verdict: APPROVE` is self-evidently false.

- [ ] id: 20261111-3 | severity: low | target: qa | kind: denial_retry_discipline
  - evidence: mdu-56 (2026-11-11, opencode): probe of the QA child's 5 shell denials (all `permission.rejected`) shows two distinct classes, and the second was handled sub-optimally despite the brief pre-stating fallbacks: `timeout 60 cp -r grok/agents /tmp/opencode/mdu56-qa-src` was denied and then retried BYTE-IDENTICAL (~1 min apart) before switching — but `cp` is an explicit content deny (`cp *` / `* cp *` in the qa block) that fires even under the `timeout *` allow, so an identical retry cannot succeed; the brief itself said "install/sed -i/script-file fallbacks are known to work if cp/heredocs/redirects get denied", i.e. the retry taught nothing the brief had not already said. Same for `timeout 120 python3 -c …` (deny `*python* -c*`) — one attempt, then the script-file fallback worked first try. By contrast the 2 FORM denials (`cd … && …; echo`, `diff -r …; echo`) were the retry-once-and-change class the shipped clauses cover (20261110-1 stays resolved — its clause C fired correctly; this is the residual the current text does not name). Cost is small (one wasted attempt) but the distinction is exactly what the shipped denial wording lacks: it teaches form-retry, not content-recognition.
  - definition change: In base prompts/qa.md (and the QA mirrors, same carrier set as 20261110-1's clause B — base prompts/qa.md, grok/agents/qa.md, qa-pro, overlay overlays/prompts/qa.md + overlay-root grok-qa.md/grok-qa-pro.md): "Denials are form-based or content-based. Form denials (compound `cd`/`;echo`/redirects) → retry ONCE in the plain single-command form. Content denials (`cp`, `mv`, `rm`, `python3 -c`, `curl`, git mutations — denied by rule regardless of wrapper) → do NOT retry; go straight to the named fallback (script file, install, file tools). Never repeat a denied command byte-identically."
  - apply-now: In the next QA brief, list the content-denied verbs explicitly next to the pre-stated probes so QA routes around them on first contact; QA-side check at verdict time: if a denied command string appears twice in the session, note it as a discipline miss (non-blocking).

  - note (2026-11-12, mdu-60): partial recurrence — QA's 4 shell denials (store probe: all permission.rejected) were FORM denials of the sha256 cross-mirror byte-parity verification, and QA escalated through three WRAPPED variants before falling back: `sed -n … | sha256sum; sed … | sha256sum; sed …` (compound) → `timeout 30 sed -n … | sha256sum` (pipe) → `timeout 30 sha256sum <(sed -n …)` (process substitution), plus one `timeout 60 python3 scripts/lib/run_ledger.py validate …; echo "RC=$?"` compound. The byte-identical-retry discipline HELD (no command repeated verbatim), but QA never dropped to the plain single-command `sha256sum <file>` form that clause C names as the retry target and that mdu-51's QA ran successfully — three attempts burned on the same denial class. No verdict harm (PASS 20/0 targeted, 925/0 full, prompt gate 7/7), so no reopen. Wording increment for the next brief batch: "After ONE form denial on a verification command, drop straight to the plain single-command form (e.g. `sha256sum <file>`, `sha256sum file1 file2`) — pipes, `;echo`, and `<()` are the SAME denial class; do not try a second wrapper." Left open.

  - note (2026-11-12, mdu-61): benign recurrence, evidence FOR the shipped clauses — QA round 1 hit 2 compound-form denials (`; echo`-wrapped commands, exactly the 20261110-1 class) and succeeded on plain-form retries on the next attempt; no byte-identical retries, no verdict harm (PASS round 1). First run where the plain-form retry landed first try after a denial. The mdu-60 wording increment (drop straight to the plain single-command form after ONE form denial) remains unshipped. Left open.

  - note (2026-11-13, mdu-59): benign recurrence with ONE discipline miss — QA's 3 shell errors (store-probed, all permission.rejected): 1 compound `cd … && git check-ignore …; echo …` (form class, by design) and 2 rows of the SAME `timeout 60 python3 -c "…datetime…"` command — the two inputs are byte-identical in the store probe, i.e. a byte-identical retry of a content-denied form (`python3 -c`), exactly what this entry's definition change forbids; the entry's own verdict-time check fires (a denied command string appearing twice = discipline miss, non-blocking). No verdict harm (GRADE PASS round 1, AC-1..AC-5 all PASS). Definition change still unapplied; the mdu-60 plain-form wording increment remains unshipped. Left open.

  - note (2026-11-13, mdu-62): qa hit 5 shell errors (repeated_tool_error warn on tool shell) — nature NOT store-probed this pass (v2 permission extraction reports none, so form-denial vs content-denial vs genuine failure is UNKNOWN, not assumed), and no verdict harm: QA graded PASS round 1 with live re-verification of all 5 ACs, so no reopen of 20261030-2 either. Because the class is unprobed, this counts as a partial recurrence of the error-volume facet, not of the byte-identical-retry defect. Apply-now increments STAND: carry the content-denied verb list and the mdu-60 "drop to the plain single-command form after ONE form denial" wording in the next QA brief. Left open.

- [x] id: 20261112-2 | severity: warn | target: orchestrator-briefs | kind: permission_denial (resolved-by-shipping 2026-11-12, mdu-60)
  - evidence: mdu-48 / sprite `ses_e6d1ac9c…` (agent `qa`): 11 rows `%Permission denied: shell%`, tool `shell` — the QA child was asked to write findings and had no write path by design (mdu-48 §S1, commit `c3b2d48`). The gap is UPSTREAM: briefs still ask a read-only grading agent to produce files, so `20261110-1` clause C (retry form) and `20261111-3` (content-denial carry) both fire against a request that should never have been made. Read-only is the grade's independence guarantee (`mdu-41`/`mdu-45` in decisions.md), so grant-write recreates the failure class and adds tree-mutation risk during validation. Applied workaround (user, 2026-11-12): the orchestrator consumed the QA reply and dispatched a write-capable agent to persist it — that becomes the default.
  - definition change: one clause, byte-identical in the three orchestrator mirrors (base `prompts/orchestrator.md`, base `grok/agents/orchestrator.md`, overlay `grok/agents/grokestrator.md`), inserted immediately after the phase-routing pre-check: "**Durable QA output (consume-and-persist):** … the orchestrator consumes the QA reply — the reply IS the deliverable — and dispatches a write-capable agent (just-code/devops) to persist it, or writes the coordination artifact itself … Do not grant QA write permission to work around this; the read-only sandbox is the grade's independence guarantee." Plus two `docs/brief-checklist.md` `## qa` lines carrying the brief pre-statement `[20261112-2]`.
  - apply-now: In every QA brief whose verdict must persist as a file, name the durable-artifact writer (just-code/devops) and pre-state verbatim "your reply is the deliverable; a write-capable agent persists artifacts; do not attempt writes." No `opencode.json` permission edit — `qa` and `qa-pro` keep the deny-first `edit` wall (AC-2 state pin).
  - note (2026-11-12, mdu-60): opened `[x]` resolved-by-shipping under the user's pre-approved in-MDU definition carve-out (no `apply the definition diffs` round-trip for this batch). Pins: base `tests/` + `test_mdu_60_qa_write_access.py` (20 cases: clause presence ×2 mirrors, cross-mirror sha256, adjacency and anchor-unchanged ×2, two checklist lines, doc consistency, AC-2 `qa`+`qa-pro`, negative boundary, net-new census, force-flag anti-fake; the overlay `grokestrator` third-mirror pins are gated on LIVE overlay-file content — skip names the pending overlay step, and they flip strict automatically once the clause lands in the overlay file). REOPEN TRIGGER: the first post-deploy QA child that attempts a write despite a brief carrying the pre-stated line, or any commit that adds a write/edit allow to `qa`/`qa-pro`.

- [ ] id: 20261112-3 | severity: warn | target: test-agent | kind: env_gated_pin
  - evidence: mdu-60 (2026-11-12, opencode): review round 1 REQUEST CHANGES (1 MEDIUM) was exactly this class — the new `tests/test_mdu_60_qa_write_access.py` pins for the THIRD mirror (overlay `grokestrator`) were gated behind env vars / fixture flags (`MDU60_TEST_ROOT`, a shipped-flag skip) instead of reading the live overlay file, so the pin could pass or skip while the real sibling file still lacked the clause; the fix child rederived the gate from live content (`OVERLAY_CLAUSE_PRESENT = HAS_OVERLAY and GROKESTRATOR.is_file() and EMITTED_CLAUSE in _text(GROKESTRATOR)`; overlay pins read the real sibling, not the test root) and the 20-case file now carries a force-flag anti-fake case. Cost: one full fresh fix child plus a second review round (round 2's residual LOW folded docs-only), i.e. review_reject_streak HIGH with 0 approvals on a run that still ended QA PASS (20/0 targeted, 925/0 full). Root cause sits with the writer, not the reviewer: the trace-review clause (20261021-11) caught it, but no test-authoring guidance forbids an env-var short-circuit on a pin. Same content-vs-form family as 20261111-3 (shell denials) — here the form-vs-content gap is in the TEST, not the command.
  - definition change: In base `skills/test-patterns/SKILL.md` (and the test-agent prompt) add: "Never gate a pin behind an env var, fixture flag, or test-root variable that can skip or satisfy it — derive every cross-mirror / live-file assertion from the live artifact's content, and give each such pin a negative case proving it fails when the live file lacks the clause (anti-fake). A check that can be satisfied without the artifact reading as present is a draft, not a gate."
  - apply-now: In the next definition-carrier MDU brief, require implement/test to state per pin: "which live file does this read, and what makes it fail if the clause is absent"; review round 1 verifies the gate reads live content rather than a flag — the exact check that cost mdu-60 its fix child.

- [x] id: 20261112-4 | severity: warn | target: qa+devops | kind: env_gap (resolved-by-shipping 2026-11-12, mdu-61)
  - evidence: mdu-48 S1/S2b (2026-10-31, sprite `ses_e6d1ac9c…`) + mdu-61 P0 re-baseline (2026-11-12): the blender MCP server can be "connected" in a harness while the Blender *application* is absent — a running `mcp-for-blender` launcher answers a live tool call with "Could not connect to Blender", and 13 stale idle launcher pairs were left behind on this machine. Zero base prompts mentioned blender, so no qa/devops child had a discipline for app-backed servers: the Playwright-or-BLOCKED rule covered the browser only, and a launcher-level "connected" status could be read as app availability. Distinct from 20261026-1 (question-tool kill) and 20261112-3 (env-gated pin): the gap was missing executor wording, not a detector or a test.
  - definition change: one clause, byte-identical in the six base carriers — qa family (`prompts/qa.md`, `grok/agents/qa.md`, `grok/agents/qa-pro.md`, after the Playwright BLOCKED anchor bullet) and devops family (`prompts/devops.md`, `grok/agents/devops.md`, `grok/agents/devops-pro.md`, last `Hard constraints:` bullet): "**App-backed MCP gating (Blender):** Before any blender-dependent step (tool call, verification item, deploy check), probe the application first: `pgrep -x blender` must show the Blender app running with the `mcp-for-blender` addon enabled. A running `mcp-for-blender` server process alone is launcher-level only and is NOT sufficient — a harness "connected" status reports the launcher, not the application. If the app is absent, immediately report a BLOCKED status (distinct from PASS/FAIL) naming the probe, what failed, and that Blender must be running with the addon enabled — mirroring the Playwright-or-BLOCKED discipline; never report blender as "connected" from launcher status alone. The same app-vs-launcher rule applies to any MCP server that fronts a running application." Plus one `docs/brief-checklist.md` line, byte-identical in `## devops` (section last line) and `## qa` (after `[20261110-1]`).
  - apply-now: In any brief with a blender-dependent step, pre-state the `pgrep -x blender` probe before the step and expect a BLOCKED-shaped report when the app is absent. No `opencode.json` or MCP config change is implied by the clause itself.
  - note (2026-11-12, mdu-61): opened `[x]` resolved-by-shipping under the user's 2026-11-12 ruling (clause text pre-approved in-MDU); scope is the BASE six carriers plus the checklist pair — the five overlay carriers are the overlay half. `docs/mcp.md` carries the reference note ("connected" is launcher-level, probe the app, launcher counts are a leak signal) so the clause has a doc anchor. Pins: `tests/test_mdu_61_mcp_fixes.py` (presence + byte-identity + adjacency + checklist adjacency + docs pins) plus the regenerated pi goldens for the four drifted templates. REOPEN TRIGGER: the first qa/devops child that reports blender as "connected" from launcher status alone, or performs a blender-dependent step without the app probe, in a brief that carried the line.

Same fail-open family as 20261111-1 (deploy.sh probe gate warn-and-continue).
  - definition change: In base `prompts/devops.md` + grok mirrors (devops, devops-pro): "After ANY config or registration mutation (`claude mcp add`, opencode.json edits, grok config writes), exit-0 is not evidence: verify the live post-state in every affected CLI (`claude mcp list`, installed-copy byte-compare against build/) and fail closed if any registration is not the pinned value — `claude mcp add` does not overwrite an existing entry (remove before add), and wrapper warnings may exit 0."
  - apply-now: In the next deploy brief, require per-CLI live post-state lines for ALL affected CLIs in the evidence file and state "a warn + exit-0 line is FAILED verification, not a pass".
